People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I, um… I came in because I keep hearing this man’s voice. It’s mostly at work, like from the garage vents, and sometimes when I’m under the hood of a car, it sounds clear—like someone is standing right next to me.

He tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. And I know that probably sounds strange, but the vents at the shop make this rattling noise anyway, and there’s this one bay where the air whistles, and I’ve told them the filters need changing for months… but this is different. It’s words. It’s him talking.

[
  {

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F25.1, F31.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I, um… I came in because I keep hearing this man’s voice. It’s mostly at work, like from the garage vents, and sometimes when I’m under the hood of a car, it sounds clear—like someone is standing right next to me.\n\nHe tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. And I know that probably sounds strange, but the vents at the shop make this rattling noise anyway, and there’s this one bay where the air whistles, and I’ve told them the filters need changing for months… but this is different. It’s words. It’s him talking.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something specific?\n\nPatient: Yeah… specific, kind of. He’ll say, like, don’t trust my husband, and not to touch certain cars. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. Like when he says not to touch a certain car, I’ll freeze up, because it sounds so clear—coming from the vent, or like right under the hood with me.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about the shop airflow, because those vents have been making noise forever, and I told them the filters—sorry. But yes, it feels hard to ignore when he’s right there telling me not to trust my husband or not to work on a car.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would harm yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: I mean… he tells me not to touch certain cars, and not to trust my husband. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town, like I’m faster than everybody in the shop, and then if my husband says something about the dishes not being done, or one of the techs moves my socket set, I just— I blow up.\n\nIt feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me down, you know? And then the voice makes it feel more… confirmed, I guess. Like I shouldn’t trust them. But sorry, the socket thing really gets me because they never put it back right, and then I’m the one wasting time looking for the 10 millimeter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely happy or energetic for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I get these times where I feel like I’m just… way up, like I could fix every car in town by myself, like nobody in the shop can keep up with me. And then it flips fast if someone gets in my way.\n\nLike if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or one of the techs moves my socket set again, I just blow up. It feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back, and I can’t stand it. The socket set thing sounds small, but it’s not when you’re moving fast and people keep messing with your tools.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.

Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… kind of. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it a “plan” like some movie thing, but it feels connected.

The voice says things and then I notice stuff at work, like the chargers or the cameras, and it all lines up in my head. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using those EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really believe that settles it.

And it’s not like—sorry, I’m thinking about how the cameras are aimed near the bays and who has access, because that matters. People act like I’m just making a big deal out of normal equipment, but normal equipment can still be used for other things. So yes, it feels like it’s part of something bigger than just random warnings.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I, um… I came in because I keep hearing this man’s voice. It’s mostly at work, like from the garage vents, and sometimes when I’m under the hood of a car, it sounds clear—like someone is standing right next to me.\n\nHe tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. And I know that probably sounds strange, but the vents at the shop make this rattling noise anyway, and there’s this one bay where the air whistles, and I’ve told them the filters need changing for months… but this is different. It’s words. It’s him talking.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something specific?\n\nPatient: Yeah… specific, kind of. He’ll say, like, don’t trust my husband, and not to touch certain cars. Especially if I’m under the hood, which is annoying because that’s literally my job, and then I start thinking maybe there’s something wrong with the wiring or the customer did something weird to it—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nBut yes. It’s a man’s voice, clear, like he’s right there by me, telling me those things.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that you feel you must do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “must” is the right word… it feels like I should listen, though. Like when he says not to touch a certain car, I’ll freeze up, because it sounds so clear—coming from the vent, or like right under the hood with me.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about the shop airflow, because those vents have been making noise forever, and I told them the filters—sorry. But yes, it feels hard to ignore when he’s right there telling me not to trust my husband or not to work on a car.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would harm yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: I mean… he tells me not to touch certain cars, and not to trust my husband. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town, like I’m faster than everybody in the shop, and then if my husband says something about the dishes not being done, or one of the techs moves my socket set, I just— I blow up.\n\nIt feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me down, you know? And then the voice makes it feel more… confirmed, I guess. Like I shouldn’t trust them. But sorry, the socket thing really gets me because they never put it back right, and then I’m the one wasting time looking for the 10 millimeter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely happy or energetic for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I get these times where I feel like I’m just… way up, like I could fix every car in town by myself, like nobody in the shop can keep up with me. And then it flips fast if someone gets in my way.\n\nLike if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or one of the techs moves my socket set again, I just blow up. It feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back, and I can’t stand it. The socket set thing sounds small, but it’s not when you’re moving fast and people keep messing with your tools.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it feels real. It’s not like a thought in my head. It comes from the garage vents, or sometimes right under the hood when I’m working, clear like a man standing next to me.\n\nAnd I know vents rattle, okay, I work around noises all day — fans, compressors, engines ticking while they cool down — I know what normal shop sounds are. This is words. He tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So… yes, I believe it’s real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your periods of feeling extremely happy or energetic are always followed by periods of feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s always like that. Sometimes I’m way up and then I just get irritated or worn out, but I don’t really track it cleanly, you know?\n\nLike, it depends what’s going on at work too. If we’ve got three cars waiting and someone put the wrong filter on the wrong ticket, then my whole day goes sideways, and then I’m not sure if I’m “down” or just mad because people don’t listen. So… I can’t say always followed by depressed. I haven’t noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you anything that you believe to be true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.\n\nLike he’s warned me about my husband and the shop owner. I’m pretty sure they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me, like checking when I’m there and what I’m doing. And I know people say the chargers are just chargers, but they’re all connected now, everything has an app or a log or some stupid cloud thing.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and said, “See, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. Footage can be cut, or they only show me the boring part. I work with electrical systems, I know how stuff can be routed around. So… yeah, the voice tells me things like that, and other people don’t believe me, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband and the shop owner are actually using the EV chargers and security cameras to monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.\n\nThe EV chargers log things, the cameras are everywhere, and it’s not hard to check when I’m there or what bay I’m near. My husband acts like I’m making it up, and the shop owner will show me some footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove it. They could leave out parts. They could show me the boring angle.\n\nSo… yes. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… it does fit together to me. I know it sounds far-fetched, I do, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.

And people keep saying, “Look, here’s the footage, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t mean anything to me. Footage can be picked through, or they show the part where nothing’s obvious. I mean, at work we deal with systems all day—wires, logs, access, who can see what—and people act like because something is normal equipment it can’t be used for something else.

So yes… the voice warning me, the chargers, the cameras, how they both act when I ask about it—it all makes sense together in my head, even if everyone else says it doesn’t.

It’s a man’s voice, clear, like he’s right there by me, telling me those things.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that you feel you must do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “must” is the right word… it feels like I should listen, though. Like when he says not to touch a certain car, I’ll freeze up, because it sounds so clear—coming from the vent, or like right under the hood with me.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about the shop airflow, because those vents have been making noise forever, and I told them the filters—sorry. But yes, it feels hard to ignore when he’s right there telling me not to trust my husband or not to work on a car.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would harm yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: I mean… he tells me not to touch certain cars, and not to trust my husband. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town, like I’m faster than everybody in the shop, and then if my husband says something about the dishes not being done, or one of the techs moves my socket set, I just— I blow up.\n\nIt feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me down, you know? And then the voice makes it feel more… confirmed, I guess. Like I shouldn’t trust them. But sorry, the socket thing really gets me because they never put it back right, and then I’m the one wasting time looking for the 10 millimeter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely happy or energetic for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I get these times where I feel like I’m just… way up, like I could fix every car in town by myself, like nobody in the shop can keep up with me. And then it flips fast if someone gets in my way.\n\nLike if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or one of the techs moves my socket set again, I just blow up. It feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back, and I can’t stand it. The socket set thing sounds small, but it’s not when you’re moving fast and people keep messing with your tools.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it feels real. It’s not like a thought in my head. It comes from the garage vents, or sometimes right under the hood when I’m working, clear like a man standing next to me.\n\nAnd I know vents rattle, okay, I work around noises all day — fans, compressors, engines ticking while they cool down — I know what normal shop sounds are. This is words. He tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So… yes, I believe it’s real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your periods of feeling extremely happy or energetic are always followed by periods of feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s always like that. Sometimes I’m way up and then I just get irritated or worn out, but I don’t really track it cleanly, you know?\n\nLike, it depends what’s going on at work too. If we’ve got three cars waiting and someone put the wrong filter on the wrong ticket, then my whole day goes sideways, and then I’m not sure if I’m “down” or just mad because people don’t listen. So… I can’t say always followed by depressed. I haven’t noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you anything that you believe to be true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.\n\nLike he’s warned me about my husband and the shop owner. I’m pretty sure they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me, like checking when I’m there and what I’m doing. And I know people say the chargers are just chargers, but they’re all connected now, everything has an app or a log or some stupid cloud thing.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and said, “See, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. Footage can be cut, or they only show me the boring part. I work with electrical systems, I know how stuff can be routed around. So… yeah, the voice tells me things like that, and other people don’t believe me, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband and the shop owner are actually using the EV chargers and security cameras to monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.\n\nThe EV chargers log things, the cameras are everywhere, and it’s not hard to check when I’m there or what bay I’m near. My husband acts like I’m making it up, and the shop owner will show me some footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove it. They could leave out parts. They could show me the boring angle.\n\nSo… yes. I believe they’re using them to monitor me, even if everyone keeps telling me there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings about your husband and the shop owner seem to be part of a larger story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it a “plan” like some movie thing, but it feels connected.\n\nThe voice says things and then I notice stuff at work, like the chargers or the cameras, and it all lines up in my head. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using those EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really believe that settles it.\n\nAnd it’s not like—sorry, I’m thinking about how the cameras are aimed near the bays and who has access, because that matters. People act like I’m just making a big deal out of normal equipment, but normal equipment can still be used for other things. So yes, it feels like it’s part of something bigger than just random warnings.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings and your beliefs about your husband and the shop owner all seem to fit together in a way that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does fit together to me. I know it sounds far-fetched, I do, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nAnd people keep saying, “Look, here’s the footage, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t mean anything to me. Footage can be picked through, or they show the part where nothing’s obvious. I mean, at work we deal with systems all day—wires, logs, access, who can see what—and people act like because something is normal equipment it can’t be used for something else.\n\nSo yes… the voice warning me, the chargers, the cameras, how they both act when I ask about it—it all makes sense together in my head, even if everyone else says it doesn’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Mostly it’s the voice. That’s what brought me in, really—it’s this man’s voice, and it comes from the garage vents sometimes, or from under the hood when I’m working on a car. It’s clear, like somebody standing right next to me, not like a thought.

If we’ve got three cars waiting and someone put the wrong filter on the wrong ticket, then my whole day goes sideways, and then I’m not sure if I’m “down” or just mad because people don’t listen. So… I can’t say always followed by depressed. I haven’t noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you anything that you believe to be true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.\n\nLike he’s warned me about my husband and the shop owner. I’m pretty sure they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me, like checking when I’m there and what I’m doing. And I know people say the chargers are just chargers, but they’re all connected now, everything has an app or a log or some stupid cloud thing.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and said, “See, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. Footage can be cut, or they only show me the boring part. I work with electrical systems, I know how stuff can be routed around. So… yeah, the voice tells me things like that, and other people don’t believe me, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband and the shop owner are actually using the EV chargers and security cameras to monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.\n\nThe EV chargers log things, the cameras are everywhere, and it’s not hard to check when I’m there or what bay I’m near. My husband acts like I’m making it up, and the shop owner will show me some footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove it. They could leave out parts. They could show me the boring angle.\n\nSo… yes. I believe they’re using them to monitor me, even if everyone keeps telling me there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings about your husband and the shop owner seem to be part of a larger story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it a “plan” like some movie thing, but it feels connected.\n\nThe voice says things and then I notice stuff at work, like the chargers or the cameras, and it all lines up in my head. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using those EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really believe that settles it.\n\nAnd it’s not like—sorry, I’m thinking about how the cameras are aimed near the bays and who has access, because that matters. People act like I’m just making a big deal out of normal equipment, but normal equipment can still be used for other things. So yes, it feels like it’s part of something bigger than just random warnings.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings and your beliefs about your husband and the shop owner all seem to fit together in a way that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does fit together to me. I know it sounds far-fetched, I do, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nAnd people keep saying, “Look, here’s the footage, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t mean anything to me. Footage can be picked through, or they show the part where nothing’s obvious. I mean, at work we deal with systems all day—wires, logs, access, who can see what—and people act like because something is normal equipment it can’t be used for something else.\n\nSo yes… the voice warning me, the chargers, the cameras, how they both act when I ask about it—it all makes sense together in my head, even if everyone else says it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is the only way you receive messages or warnings, or are there other ways you get information that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the voice. That’s what brought me in, really—it’s this man’s voice, and it comes from the garage vents sometimes, or from under the hood when I’m working on a car. It’s clear, like somebody standing right next to me, not like a thought.\n\nAnd it’ll say not to trust my husband, or tell me not to touch certain cars. Which is hard because that’s my job, you know, I can’t just stand there with a work order in my hand forever. There was this one time I had the hood up and I kept thinking maybe it was a belt noise or something echoing through the bay, because sound travels weird in a shop, but no… it was words. The vents, under the hood… that’s where it happens.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think the voice, the EV chargers, and the security cameras are all connected as part of something bigger that's happening to you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / automotive_service_technician_or_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing, even when they show me the footage and say there’s nothing there.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, yes, I think they’re connected. I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, like I’m making some giant story out of chargers and cameras, but I’m not stupid—I work around this stuff every day. Equipment can do more than people admit it can.

I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep track of me. They’ll show me footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. People can choose what footage to show. Or leave things out.

And the chargers—sorry, this is what I keep trying to explain—those things aren’t just plugs, there’s data, there’s access, there’s who installed what and who can see what. Like at the shop, we had this whole thing once with a scan tool not syncing right, and everyone blamed the Wi-Fi, but it was actually permissions, and nobody listened to me at first—

But yes. The voice, the chargers, the cameras… it all feels like part of the same bigger thing happening to me.

He’ll say, like, don’t trust my husband, and not to touch certain cars. Like when he says not to touch a certain car, I’ll freeze up, because it sounds so clear—coming from the vent, or like right under the hood with me.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about the shop airflow, because those vents have been making noise forever, and I told them the filters—sorry. But yes, it feels hard to ignore when he’s right there telling me not to trust my husband or not to work on a car.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would harm yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: I mean… he tells me not to touch certain cars, and not to trust my husband. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town, like I’m faster than everybody in the shop, and then if my husband says something about the dishes not being done, or one of the techs moves my socket set, I just— I blow up.\n\nIt feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me down, you know? And then the voice makes it feel more… confirmed, I guess. Like I shouldn’t trust them. But sorry, the socket thing really gets me because they never put it back right, and then I’m the one wasting time looking for the 10 millimeter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely happy or energetic for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I get these times where I feel like I’m just… way up, like I could fix every car in town by myself, like nobody in the shop can keep up with me. And then it flips fast if someone gets in my way.\n\nLike if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or one of the techs moves my socket set again, I just blow up. It feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back, and I can’t stand it. The socket set thing sounds small, but it’s not when you’re moving fast and people keep messing with your tools.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it feels real. It’s not like a thought in my head. It comes from the garage vents, or sometimes right under the hood when I’m working, clear like a man standing next to me.\n\nAnd I know vents rattle, okay, I work around noises all day — fans, compressors, engines ticking while they cool down — I know what normal shop sounds are. This is words. He tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. Sometimes I’m way up and then I just get irritated or worn out, but I don’t really track it cleanly, you know?\n\nLike, it depends what’s going on at work too. If we’ve got three cars waiting and someone put the wrong filter on the wrong ticket, then my whole day goes sideways, and then I’m not sure if I’m “down” or just mad because people don’t listen. So… I can’t say always followed by depressed. I haven’t noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you anything that you believe to be true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.\n\nLike he’s warned me about my husband and the shop owner. I’m pretty sure they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me, like checking when I’m there and what I’m doing. And I know people say the chargers are just chargers, but they’re all connected now, everything has an app or a log or some stupid cloud thing.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and said, “See, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. Footage can be cut, or they only show me the boring part. I work with electrical systems, I know how stuff can be routed around. So… yeah, the voice tells me things like that, and other people don’t believe me, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband and the shop owner are actually using the EV chargers and security cameras to monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.\n\nThe EV chargers log things, the cameras are everywhere, and it’s not hard to check when I’m there or what bay I’m near. My husband acts like I’m making it up, and the shop owner will show me some footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove it. They could leave out parts. They could show me the boring angle.\n\nSo… yes. I believe they’re using them to monitor me, even if everyone keeps telling me there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings about your husband and the shop owner seem to be part of a larger story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it a “plan” like some movie thing, but it feels connected.\n\nThe voice says things and then I notice stuff at work, like the chargers or the cameras, and it all lines up in my head. No, it’s not random. I mean… I know people keep telling me I’m connecting things that don’t go together, but it feels very specifically about me.

But yes, it feels hard to ignore when he’s right there telling me not to trust my husband or not to work on a car.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would harm yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: I mean… he tells me not to touch certain cars, and not to trust my husband. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town, like I’m faster than everybody in the shop, and then if my husband says something about the dishes not being done, or one of the techs moves my socket set, I just— I blow up.\n\nIt feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me down, you know? And then the voice makes it feel more… confirmed, I guess. Like I shouldn’t trust them. But sorry, the socket thing really gets me because they never put it back right, and then I’m the one wasting time looking for the 10 millimeter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely happy or energetic for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I get these times where I feel like I’m just… way up, like I could fix every car in town by myself, like nobody in the shop can keep up with me. And then it flips fast if someone gets in my way.\n\nLike if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or one of the techs moves my socket set again, I just blow up. It feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back, and I can’t stand it. The socket set thing sounds small, but it’s not when you’re moving fast and people keep messing with your tools.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it feels real. It’s not like a thought in my head. It comes from the garage vents, or sometimes right under the hood when I’m working, clear like a man standing next to me.\n\nAnd I know vents rattle, okay, I work around noises all day — fans, compressors, engines ticking while they cool down — I know what normal shop sounds are. This is words. He tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So… yes, I believe it’s real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your periods of feeling extremely happy or energetic are always followed by periods of feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s always like that. Sometimes I’m way up and then I just get irritated or worn out, but I don’t really track it cleanly, you know?\n\nLike, it depends what’s going on at work too. If we’ve got three cars waiting and someone put the wrong filter on the wrong ticket, then my whole day goes sideways, and then I’m not sure if I’m “down” or just mad because people don’t listen. So… I can’t say always followed by depressed. I haven’t noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you anything that you believe to be true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.\n\nLike he’s warned me about my husband and the shop owner. I’m pretty sure they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me, like checking when I’m there and what I’m doing. And I know people say the chargers are just chargers, but they’re all connected now, everything has an app or a log or some stupid cloud thing.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and said, “See, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. Footage can be cut, or they only show me the boring part. I work with electrical systems, I know how stuff can be routed around. So… yeah, the voice tells me things like that, and other people don’t believe me, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband and the shop owner are actually using the EV chargers and security cameras to monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.\n\nThe EV chargers log things, the cameras are everywhere, and it’s not hard to check when I’m there or what bay I’m near. My husband acts like I’m making it up, and the shop owner will show me some footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove it. They could leave out parts. They could show me the boring angle.\n\nSo… yes. I believe they’re using them to monitor me, even if everyone keeps telling me there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings about your husband and the shop owner seem to be part of a larger story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it a “plan” like some movie thing, but it feels connected.\n\nThe voice says things and then I notice stuff at work, like the chargers or the cameras, and it all lines up in my head. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using those EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really believe that settles it.\n\nAnd it’s not like—sorry, I’m thinking about how the cameras are aimed near the bays and who has access, because that matters. People act like I’m just making a big deal out of normal equipment, but normal equipment can still be used for other things. So yes, it feels like it’s part of something bigger than just random warnings.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings and your beliefs about your husband and the shop owner all seem to fit together in a way that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does fit together to me. I know it sounds far-fetched, I do, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nAnd people keep saying, “Look, here’s the footage, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t mean anything to me. Footage can be picked through, or they show the part where nothing’s obvious. I mean, at work we deal with systems all day—wires, logs, access, who can see what—and people act like because something is normal equipment it can’t be used for something else.\n\nSo yes… the voice warning me, the chargers, the cameras, how they both act when I ask about it—it all makes sense together in my head, even if everyone else says it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is the only way you receive messages or warnings, or are there other ways you get information that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the voice. That’s what brought me in, really—it’s this man’s voice, and it comes from the garage vents sometimes, or from under the hood when I’m working on a car. It’s clear, like somebody standing right next to me, not like a thought.\n\nAnd it’ll say not to trust my husband, or tell me not to touch certain cars. Which is hard because that’s my job, you know, I can’t just stand there with a work order in my hand forever. There was this one time I had the hood up and I kept thinking maybe it was a belt noise or something echoing through the bay, because sound travels weird in a shop, but no… it was words. Clear words.\n\nSo, yeah, the voice is the main way I get warnings. The vents, under the hood… that’s where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice, the EV chargers, and the security cameras are all connected as part of something bigger that's happening to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think they’re connected. I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, like I’m making some giant story out of chargers and cameras, but I’m not stupid—I work around this stuff every day. Equipment can do more than people admit it can.\n\nI’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep track of me. They’ll show me footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. People can choose what footage to show. Or leave things out.\n\nAnd the chargers—sorry, this is what I keep trying to explain—those things aren’t just plugs, there’s data, there’s access, there’s who installed what and who can see what. Like at the shop, we had this whole thing once with a scan tool not syncing right, and everyone blamed the Wi-Fi, but it was actually permissions, and nobody listened to me at first—\n\nBut yes. The voice, the chargers, the cameras… it all feels like part of the same bigger thing happening to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voice or the events at your workplace are specifically related to you or are they just random occurrences?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not random. I mean… I know people keep telling me I’m connecting things that don’t go together, but it feels very specifically about me.\n\nThe chargers, the cameras, the way my husband and the shop owner act—like when they show me footage and say, “See, nothing’s happening,” that doesn’t settle it for me. I mean… I can say maybe there’s another explanation because that’s what people want me to say, and I’m trying to be reasonable. I know it sounds far-fetched when I hear myself saying it in here.

But no, I don’t really feel like it’s just random or misunderstood. I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me the footage and say, “Look, nothing’s there,” that doesn’t prove it to me. Footage can be picked over, edited, or just not show the part they don’t want me to see.

And the chargers—people act like they’re just for charging cars, but there’s systems behind them, data, access, all that. At the shop we’ve had equipment do things nobody expected because somebody had the wrong permissions set, and then everyone acted like it was obvious after I figured it out. So… I know how it sounds, but I don’t feel like my interpretation is wrong.

Especially if I’m under the hood, which is annoying because that’s literally my job, and then I start thinking maybe there’s something wrong with the wiring or the customer did something weird to it—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nBut yes. It’s a man’s voice, clear, like he’s right there by me, telling me those things.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that you feel you must do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “must” is the right word… it feels like I should listen, though. Like when he says not to touch a certain car, I’ll freeze up, because it sounds so clear—coming from the vent, or like right under the hood with me.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about the shop airflow, because those vents have been making noise forever, and I told them the filters—sorry. But yes, it feels hard to ignore when he’s right there telling me not to trust my husband or not to work on a car.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would harm yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: I mean… he tells me not to touch certain cars, and not to trust my husband. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town, like I’m faster than everybody in the shop, and then if my husband says something about the dishes not being done, or one of the techs moves my socket set, I just— I blow up.\n\nIt feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me down, you know? And then the voice makes it feel more… confirmed, I guess. Like I shouldn’t trust them. But sorry, the socket thing really gets me because they never put it back right, and then I’m the one wasting time looking for the 10 millimeter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely happy or energetic for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I get these times where I feel like I’m just… way up, like I could fix every car in town by myself, like nobody in the shop can keep up with me. And then it flips fast if someone gets in my way.\n\nLike if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or one of the techs moves my socket set again, I just blow up. It feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back, and I can’t stand it. The socket set thing sounds small, but it’s not when you’re moving fast and people keep messing with your tools.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it feels real. It’s not like a thought in my head. It comes from the garage vents, or sometimes right under the hood when I’m working, clear like a man standing next to me.\n\nAnd I know vents rattle, okay, I work around noises all day — fans, compressors, engines ticking while they cool down — I know what normal shop sounds are. This is words. He tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So… yes, I believe it’s real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your periods of feeling extremely happy or energetic are always followed by periods of feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s always like that. Sometimes I’m way up and then I just get irritated or worn out, but I don’t really track it cleanly, you know?\n\nLike, it depends what’s going on at work too. If we’ve got three cars waiting and someone put the wrong filter on the wrong ticket, then my whole day goes sideways, and then I’m not sure if I’m “down” or just mad because people don’t listen. So… I can’t say always followed by depressed. I haven’t noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you anything that you believe to be true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.\n\nLike he’s warned me about my husband and the shop owner. I’m pretty sure they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me, like checking when I’m there and what I’m doing. And I know people say the chargers are just chargers, but they’re all connected now, everything has an app or a log or some stupid cloud thing.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and said, “See, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. Footage can be cut, or they only show me the boring part. I work with electrical systems, I know how stuff can be routed around. So… yeah, the voice tells me things like that, and other people don’t believe me, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband and the shop owner are actually using the EV chargers and security cameras to monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.\n\nThe EV chargers log things, the cameras are everywhere, and it’s not hard to check when I’m there or what bay I’m near. My husband acts like I’m making it up, and the shop owner will show me some footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove it. They could leave out parts. They could show me the boring angle.\n\nSo… yes. I believe they’re using them to monitor me, even if everyone keeps telling me there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings about your husband and the shop owner seem to be part of a larger story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it a “plan” like some movie thing, but it feels connected.\n\nThe voice says things and then I notice stuff at work, like the chargers or the cameras, and it all lines up in my head. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using those EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really believe that settles it.\n\nAnd it’s not like—sorry, I’m thinking about how the cameras are aimed near the bays and who has access, because that matters. People act like I’m just making a big deal out of normal equipment, but normal equipment can still be used for other things. So yes, it feels like it’s part of something bigger than just random warnings.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings and your beliefs about your husband and the shop owner all seem to fit together in a way that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does fit together to me. I know it sounds far-fetched, I do, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nAnd people keep saying, “Look, here’s the footage, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t mean anything to me. Footage can be picked through, or they show the part where nothing’s obvious. I mean, at work we deal with systems all day—wires, logs, access, who can see what—and people act like because something is normal equipment it can’t be used for something else.\n\nSo yes… the voice warning me, the chargers, the cameras, how they both act when I ask about it—it all makes sense together in my head, even if everyone else says it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is the only way you receive messages or warnings, or are there other ways you get information that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the voice. That’s what brought me in, really—it’s this man’s voice, and it comes from the garage vents sometimes, or from under the hood when I’m working on a car. It’s clear, like somebody standing right next to me, not like a thought.\n\nAnd it’ll say not to trust my husband, or tell me not to touch certain cars. Which is hard because that’s my job, you know, I can’t just stand there with a work order in my hand forever. There was this one time I had the hood up and I kept thinking maybe it was a belt noise or something echoing through the bay, because sound travels weird in a shop, but no… it was words. Clear words.\n\nSo, yeah, the voice is the main way I get warnings. The vents, under the hood… that’s where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice, the EV chargers, and the security cameras are all connected as part of something bigger that's happening to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think they’re connected. I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, like I’m making some giant story out of chargers and cameras, but I’m not stupid—I work around this stuff every day. Equipment can do more than people admit it can.\n\nI’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep track of me. They’ll show me footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. People can choose what footage to show. Or leave things out.\n\nAnd the chargers—sorry, this is what I keep trying to explain—those things aren’t just plugs, there’s data, there’s access, there’s who installed what and who can see what. Like at the shop, we had this whole thing once with a scan tool not syncing right, and everyone blamed the Wi-Fi, but it was actually permissions, and nobody listened to me at first—\n\nBut yes. The voice, the chargers, the cameras… it all feels like part of the same bigger thing happening to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voice or the events at your workplace are specifically related to you or are they just random occurrences?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not random. I mean… I know people keep telling me I’m connecting things that don’t go together, but it feels very specifically about me.\n\nThe chargers, the cameras, the way my husband and the shop owner act—like when they show me footage and say, “See, nothing’s happening,” that doesn’t settle it for me. Footage can be chosen. You don’t have to show everything. And at work, people think because it’s just shop equipment it can’t be used for anything else, but that’s not how systems work.\n\nSorry, I know I’m going off a little. But yes… I believe it’s aimed at me. I’m still convinced they’re using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that there could be another explanation for the things the voice is telling you, or do you feel certain that your interpretation is the only correct one?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can say maybe there’s another explanation because that’s what people want me to say, and I’m trying to be reasonable. I know it sounds far-fetched when I hear myself saying it in here.\n\nBut no, I don’t really feel like it’s just random or misunderstood. I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me the footage and say, “Look, nothing’s there,” that doesn’t prove it to me. Footage can be picked over, edited, or just not show the part they don’t want me to see.\n\nAnd the chargers—people act like they’re just for charging cars, but there’s systems behind them, data, access, all that. At the shop we’ve had equipment do things nobody expected because somebody had the wrong permissions set, and then everyone acted like it was obvious after I figured it out. So… I know how it sounds, but I don’t feel like my interpretation is wrong.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I don’t know what “definitive” would even mean, honestly. Like, they already show me footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” and everyone acts like that should settle it, but it doesn’t. Footage is just what someone chooses to show you.

He’ll say, like, don’t trust my husband, and not to touch certain cars. Especially if I’m under the hood, which is annoying because that’s literally my job, and then I start thinking maybe there’s something wrong with the wiring or the customer did something weird to it—sorry, that’s not what you asked.\n\nBut yes. It’s a man’s voice, clear, like he’s right there by me, telling me those things.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that you feel you must do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “must” is the right word… it feels like I should listen, though. Like when he says not to touch a certain car, I’ll freeze up, because it sounds so clear—coming from the vent, or like right under the hood with me.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about the shop airflow, because those vents have been making noise forever, and I told them the filters—sorry. But yes, it feels hard to ignore when he’s right there telling me not to trust my husband or not to work on a car.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something that would harm yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: I mean… he tells me not to touch certain cars, and not to trust my husband. It’s not like he says, “hurt yourself” or “hurt someone,” not exactly. But when it’s coming through the vents like that, or right under the hood while I’m trying to work, it feels… it feels serious, like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nThe garage has these vents that rattle all day, and there’s one bay where the air whistles, and I know people think it’s just noise, but this is words. A man’s voice. Clear, like somebody standing next to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes significantly when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my mood does shift, but it’s not always just because of the voice, it’s like it catches me when I’m already up there. I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town, like I’m faster than everybody in the shop, and then if my husband says something about the dishes not being done, or one of the techs moves my socket set, I just— I blow up.\n\nIt feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me down, you know? And then the voice makes it feel more… confirmed, I guess. Like I shouldn’t trust them. But sorry, the socket thing really gets me because they never put it back right, and then I’m the one wasting time looking for the 10 millimeter.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel extremely happy or energetic for periods of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I get these times where I feel like I’m just… way up, like I could fix every car in town by myself, like nobody in the shop can keep up with me. And then it flips fast if someone gets in my way.\n\nLike if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, or one of the techs moves my socket set again, I just blow up. It feels like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back, and I can’t stand it. The socket set thing sounds small, but it’s not when you’re moving fast and people keep messing with your tools.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is real?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, it feels real. It’s not like a thought in my head. It comes from the garage vents, or sometimes right under the hood when I’m working, clear like a man standing next to me.\n\nAnd I know vents rattle, okay, I work around noises all day — fans, compressors, engines ticking while they cool down — I know what normal shop sounds are. This is words. He tells me not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So… yes, I believe it’s real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your periods of feeling extremely happy or energetic are always followed by periods of feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s always like that. Sometimes I’m way up and then I just get irritated or worn out, but I don’t really track it cleanly, you know?\n\nLike, it depends what’s going on at work too. If we’ve got three cars waiting and someone put the wrong filter on the wrong ticket, then my whole day goes sideways, and then I’m not sure if I’m “down” or just mad because people don’t listen. So… I can’t say always followed by depressed. I haven’t noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you anything that you believe to be true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Yeah… he tells me things that I know sound, like, ridiculous if I say them out loud. But I still think they’re true.\n\nLike he’s warned me about my husband and the shop owner. I’m pretty sure they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me, like checking when I’m there and what I’m doing. And I know people say the chargers are just chargers, but they’re all connected now, everything has an app or a log or some stupid cloud thing.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and said, “See, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. Footage can be cut, or they only show me the boring part. I work with electrical systems, I know how stuff can be routed around. So… yeah, the voice tells me things like that, and other people don’t believe me, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your husband and the shop owner are actually using the EV chargers and security cameras to monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I know how it sounds, okay? Like if somebody at the counter told me that, I’d probably think they were stressed or watching too many videos online. But I’m still convinced they’re doing it.\n\nThe EV chargers log things, the cameras are everywhere, and it’s not hard to check when I’m there or what bay I’m near. My husband acts like I’m making it up, and the shop owner will show me some footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove it. They could leave out parts. They could show me the boring angle.\n\nSo… yes. I believe they’re using them to monitor me, even if everyone keeps telling me there’s nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings about your husband and the shop owner seem to be part of a larger story or plan to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it a “plan” like some movie thing, but it feels connected.\n\nThe voice says things and then I notice stuff at work, like the chargers or the cameras, and it all lines up in my head. I know it sounds far-fetched, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using those EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me footage and say there’s nothing there, I don’t really believe that settles it.\n\nAnd it’s not like—sorry, I’m thinking about how the cameras are aimed near the bays and who has access, because that matters. People act like I’m just making a big deal out of normal equipment, but normal equipment can still be used for other things. So yes, it feels like it’s part of something bigger than just random warnings.\n\nDoctor: Do the voice's warnings and your beliefs about your husband and the shop owner all seem to fit together in a way that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does fit together to me. I know it sounds far-fetched, I do, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nAnd people keep saying, “Look, here’s the footage, nothing’s happening,” but that doesn’t mean anything to me. Footage can be picked through, or they show the part where nothing’s obvious. I mean, at work we deal with systems all day—wires, logs, access, who can see what—and people act like because something is normal equipment it can’t be used for something else.\n\nSo yes… the voice warning me, the chargers, the cameras, how they both act when I ask about it—it all makes sense together in my head, even if everyone else says it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice is the only way you receive messages or warnings, or are there other ways you get information that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the voice. That’s what brought me in, really—it’s this man’s voice, and it comes from the garage vents sometimes, or from under the hood when I’m working on a car. It’s clear, like somebody standing right next to me, not like a thought.\n\nAnd it’ll say not to trust my husband, or tell me not to touch certain cars. Which is hard because that’s my job, you know, I can’t just stand there with a work order in my hand forever. There was this one time I had the hood up and I kept thinking maybe it was a belt noise or something echoing through the bay, because sound travels weird in a shop, but no… it was words. Clear words.\n\nSo, yeah, the voice is the main way I get warnings. The vents, under the hood… that’s where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice, the EV chargers, and the security cameras are all connected as part of something bigger that's happening to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, I think they’re connected. I know how it sounds when I say it out loud, like I’m making some giant story out of chargers and cameras, but I’m not stupid—I work around this stuff every day. Equipment can do more than people admit it can.\n\nI’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep track of me. They’ll show me footage and say, “See, nothing’s there,” but that doesn’t prove anything to me. People can choose what footage to show. Or leave things out.\n\nAnd the chargers—sorry, this is what I keep trying to explain—those things aren’t just plugs, there’s data, there’s access, there’s who installed what and who can see what. Like at the shop, we had this whole thing once with a scan tool not syncing right, and everyone blamed the Wi-Fi, but it was actually permissions, and nobody listened to me at first—\n\nBut yes. The voice, the chargers, the cameras… it all feels like part of the same bigger thing happening to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voice or the events at your workplace are specifically related to you or are they just random occurrences?\n\nPatient: No, it’s not random. But yes… I believe it’s aimed at me. I’m still convinced they’re using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that there could be another explanation for the things the voice is telling you, or do you feel certain that your interpretation is the only correct one?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can say maybe there’s another explanation because that’s what people want me to say, and I’m trying to be reasonable. I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. Because people keep saying, “That’s not happening,” and they show me footage like that’s supposed to prove it, but I still feel like there are pieces missing. I know it sounds far-fetched, the EV chargers and the cameras and all that, but I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using them to track what I’m doing.

And trust is hard with this, because even if I trust the person, how do I know what they were shown? Or what they weren’t shown? Like at the shop, I’ve seen systems look totally normal until you check the settings behind it—sorry, I’m wandering. But no… I don’t think just being told it isn’t true would make me stop believing it.

---------- turn 18 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
